course"</figcaption></figure> <p id="mwDQ">A <b id="mwDg">massive open online course</b> (<b id="mwDw">MOOC</b> <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap" about="#mwt7" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwEA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"IPAc-en","href":"./Template:IPAc-en"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"m"},"2":{"wt":"uː"},"3":{"wt":"k"}},"i":0}}]}'><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="'m' in 'my'">m</span><span title="/uː/: 'oo' in 'goose'">uː</span><span title="'k' in 'kind'">k</span></span>/</a></span></span>) or an <b id="mwEQ">open online course</b> is an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Online_course" title="Online course" class="mw-redirect" id="mwEg">online course</a> aimed at unlimited participation and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_access" title="Open access" id="mwEw">open access</a> via the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Web" title="World Wide Web" id="mwFA">Web</a>.<sup about="#mwt10" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwFQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwFg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwFw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwGA">]</span></span></a></sup> In addition to traditional course materials, such as filmed lectures, readings, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Problem_set" title="Problem set" id="mwGQ">problem sets</a>, many MOOCs provide <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interactive_course" title="Interactive course" id="mwGg">interactive courses</a> with user forums or social media discussions to support community interactions among students, professors, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teaching_assistant" title="Teaching assistant" id="mwGw">teaching assistants</a> (TAs), as well as immediate feedback to quick quizzes and assignments. MOOCs are a widely researched development in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distance_education" title="Distance education" id="mwHA">distance education</a>,<sup about="#mwt11" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-CFP_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"CFP"}}'><a href="#cite_note-CFP-2" id="mwHQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwHg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwHw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIA">]</span></span></a></sup> first introduced in 2008,<sup about="#mwt12" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-3" id="mwIQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwIg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIw">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwJA">]</span></span></a></sup> that emerged as a popular mode of learning in 2012.<sup about="#mwt15" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYTimes030613_4-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYTimes030613"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYTimes030613-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYTimes030613-4" id="mwJQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwJg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwJw">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt18" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_5-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:1-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-5" id="mwKQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwKg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKw">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLA">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwLQ">Early MOOCs (cMOOCs: Connectivist MOOCs) often emphasized open-access features, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_content" title="Open content" class="mw-redirect" id="mwLg">open licensing</a> of content, structure and learning goals, to promote the reuse and remixing of resources. Some later MOOCs (xMOOCs: extended MOOCs) use closed licenses for their course materials while maintaining free access for students.<sup about="#mwt20" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MOOC_Misnomer_6-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MOOC Misnomer"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-MOOC_Misnomer-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MOOC_Misnomer-6" id="mwLw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMQ">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt23" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwMw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt26" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-RemixInfoWeek_8-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"RemixInfoWeek"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-RemixInfoWeek-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-RemixInfoWeek-8" id="mwNw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwOA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOQ">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt28" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-in_AIS_Electronic_Library_AISeL_9-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"in AIS Electronic Library AISeL"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-in_AIS_Electronic_Library_AISeL-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-in_AIS_Electronic_Library_AISeL-9" id="mwOw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwPA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPQ">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPg">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt29" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwPw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"toclimit","href":"./Template:Toclimit"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"3"}},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r886046785" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt30" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:TOC limit/styles.css"}}'>.mw-parser-output .toclimit-2 .toclevel-1 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-3 .toclevel-2 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-4 .toclevel-3 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-5 .toclevel-4 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-6 .toclevel-5 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-7 .toclevel-6 ul{display:none}</style></span><div class="toclimit-3" about="#mwt29" id="mwQA"><meta property="mw:PageProp/toc"/></div> </section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwQQ" aria-labelledby="History"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=1" title="Edit section: History"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwQg"><span id="mwQw"><video poster="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/What_is_a_MOOC.ogv/250px--What_is_a_MOOC.ogv.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser" controls="" preload="none" data-mw-tmh="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/14/What_is_a_MOOC.ogv?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=index&utm_content=original" type='video/ogg; codecs="theora, vorbis"' data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="720" id="mwRQ"/><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/1/14/What_is_a_MOOC.ogv/What_is_a_MOOC.ogv.240p.vp9.webm" type='video/webm; data-transcodekey="480p.vp9.webm" id="mwRw"/></video></span><figcaption id="mwSA">What is a MOOC?, December 2010</figcaption></figure> <section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwSQ" aria-labelledby="Precursors"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Precursors">Precursors</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=2" title="Edit section: Precursors"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt31" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwSg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Distance learning"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1353705441" about="#mwt32" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt31" id="mwSw">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distance_learning" title="Distance learning" class="mw-redirect">Distance learning</a></div> <p id="mwTA">Before the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_Age" title="Digital Age" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTQ">Digital Age</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distance_learning" title="Distance learning" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTg">distance learning</a> appeared in the form of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Correspondence_courses" title="Correspondence courses" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTw">correspondence courses</a> in the 1890s–1920s and later radio and television broadcast of courses and early forms of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-learning" title="E-learning" class="mw-redirect" id="mwUA">e-learning</a>. Typically fewer than five percent of the students would complete a course.<sup about="#mwt35" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:5_10-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":5"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:5-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:5-10" id="mwUQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwUg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUw">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVA">]</span></span></a></sup> For example the Stanford Honors Cooperative Program, established in 1954, eventually offered video classes on-site at companies, at night, leading to a fully accredited Master's degree. This program was controversial because the companies paid double the normal tuition paid by full-time students.<sup about="#mwt38" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:6_11-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":6"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:6-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:6-11" id="mwVQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVw">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWA">]</span></span></a></sup> The 2000s saw changes in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-learning" title="E-learning" class="mw-redirect" id="mwWQ">online, or e-learning</a> and distance education, with increasing online presence, open learning opportunities, and the development of MOOCs.<sup about="#mwt41" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-blogs.cetis.org.uk_12-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"blogs.cetis.org.uk"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-blogs.cetis.org.uk-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-blogs.cetis.org.uk-12" id="mwWg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwWw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXA">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXQ">]</span></span></a></sup> By 2010 audiences for the most popular college courses such as "Justice" with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_J._Sandel" title="Michael J. Sandel" class="mw-redirect" id="mwXg">Michael J. Sandel</a> and "Human Anatomy" with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marian_Diamond" title="Marian Diamond" id="mwXw">Marian Diamond</a> were reaching millions.<sup about="#mwt44" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:7_13-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":7"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:7-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:7-13" id="mwYA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYg">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYw">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwZA" aria-labelledby="Early_approaches"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Early_approaches">Early approaches</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=3" title="Edit section: Early approaches"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwZQ"><span id="mwZg"><video poster="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/George_Siemens_interview_on_MOOCs_and_Open_Education.webm/250px--George_Siemens_interview_on_MOOCs_and_Open_Education.webm.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser" controls="" preload="none" data-mw-tmh="" height="141" width="250" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:George_Siemens_interview_on_MOOCs_and_Open_Education.webm" data-durationhint="659" class="mw-file-element" id="mwZw"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/34/George_Siemens_interview_on_MOOCs_and_Open_Education.webm?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=index&utm_content=original" type='video/webm; codecs="vp8, vorbis"' data-file-width="568" data-file-height="320" id="mwaA"/><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/3/34/George_Siemens_interview_on_MOOCs_and_Open_Education.webm/George_Siemens_interview_on_MOOCs_and_Open_Education.webm.240p.vp9.webm" type='video/webm; codecs="vp9, opus"' data-width="426" data-height="240" data-transcodekey="240p.vp9.webm" id="mwaQ"/></video></span><figcaption id="mwag">George Siemens interview</figcaption></figure> <p id="mwaw">The first MOOCs emerged from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_educational_resources" title="Open educational resources" id="mwbA">open educational resources</a> (OER) movement, which was sparked by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIT_OpenCourseWare" title="MIT OpenCourseWare" id="mwbQ">MIT OpenCourseWare</a> project.<sup about="#mwt47" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:8_14-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":8"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:8-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:8-14" id="mwbg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwbw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcA">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcQ">]</span></span></a></sup> The OER movement was motivated from work by researchers who pointed out that class size and learning outcomes had no established connection. Here, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Barwick" title="Daniel Barwick" id="mwcg">Daniel Barwick</a>'s work is the most often-cited example.<sup about="#mwt50" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:9_15-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":9"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:9-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:9-15" id="mwcw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwdA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdQ">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt53" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-16" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwdw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mweA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mweQ">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mweg">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwew">Within the OER movement, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikiversity" title="Wikiversity" id="mwfA">Wikiversity</a> was founded in 2006 and the first open course on the platform was organised in 2007. A ten-week course with more than 70 students was used to test the idea of making Wikiversity an open and free platform for education in the tradition of Scandinavian free adult education, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_high_school" title="Folk high school" id="mwfQ">Folk High School</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_school_movement" title="Free school movement" id="mwfg">free school movement</a>.<sup about="#mwt56" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-17" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-17" id="mwfw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgQ">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgg">]</span></span></a></sup> The term <i id="mwgw">MOOC</i> was coined in 2008 by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Cormier?action=edit&redlink=1" title="Dave Cormier (page does not exist)" class="new" typeof="mw:LocalizedAttrs" id="mwhA" data-mw-i18n='{"title":{"lang":"x-page","key":"red-link-title","params":["Dave Cormier"]}}'>Dave Cormier</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thompson_Rivers_University" title="Thompson Rivers University" id="mwhQ">Thompson Rivers University</a> in response to a course called <i id="mwhg">Connectivism and Connective Knowledge</i> (also known as <i id="mwhw">CCK08</i>). CCK08, which was led by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Siemens" title="George Siemens" id="mwiA">George Siemens</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabasca_University" title="Athabasca University" id="mwiQ">Athabasca University</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Downes" title="Stephen Downes" id="mwig">Stephen Downes</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Research_Council_(Canada)" title="National Research Council (Canada)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwiw">National Research Council</a>, consisted of 25 tuition-paying students in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuing_education" title="Continuing education" id="mwjA">Extended Education</a> at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Manitoba" title="University of Manitoba" id="mwjQ">University of Manitoba</a>, as well as over 2200 online students from the general public who paid nothing.<sup about="#mwt59" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-THE_18-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"THE"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-THE-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-THE-18" id="mwjg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkA">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkQ">]</span></span></a></sup> All course content was available through <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RSS" title="RSS" id="mwkg">RSS</a> feeds, and online students could participate through collaborative tools, including blog posts, threaded discussions in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moodle" title="Moodle" id="mwkw">Moodle</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Life" title="Second Life" id="mwlA">Second Life</a> meetings.<sup about="#mwt62" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-downes_19-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"downes"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-downes-19"}}'><a href="#cite_note-downes-19" id="mwlQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwlg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlw">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt66" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-auto_20-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"auto"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-auto-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-auto-20" id="mwmQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwmg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmw">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwnA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt69" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-auto1_21-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"auto1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-auto1-21"}}'><a href="#cite_note-auto1-21" id="mwnQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwng"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwnw">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwoA">]</span></span></a></sup> Stephen Downes considers these so-called cMOOCs to be more "creative and dynamic" than the current xMOOCs, which he believes "resemble television shows or digital textbooks".<sup about="#mwt70" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-THE_18-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"THE"}}'><a href="#cite_note-THE-18" id="mwoQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwog"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwow">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpA">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwpQ">Other cMOOCs were then developed; for example, Jim Groom from The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Mary_Washington" title="University of Mary Washington" id="mwpg">University of Mary Washington</a> and Michael Branson Smith of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/York_College,_City_University_of_New_York" title="York College, City University of New York" id="mwpw">York College, City University of New York</a> hosted MOOCs through several universities starting with 2011's 'Digital Storytelling' (ds106) MOOC.<sup about="#mwt73" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-GROOM_22-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"GROOM"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-GROOM-22"}}'><a href="#cite_note-GROOM-22" id="mwqA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwqQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqg">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqw">]</span></span></a></sup> MOOCs from private, non-profit institutions emphasized prominent faculty members and expanded existing distance learning offerings (e.g., podcasts) into free and open online courses.<sup about="#mwt75" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:10_23-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":10"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:10-23"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:10-23" id="mwrA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwrQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrg">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrw">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwsA">Alongside the development of these open courses, other E-learning platforms emerged – such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khan_Academy" title="Khan Academy" id="mwsQ">Khan Academy</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peer-to-Peer_University" title="Peer-to-Peer University" class="mw-redirect" id="mwsg">Peer-to-Peer University</a> (P2PU), <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udemy" title="Udemy" id="mwsw">Udemy</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ALISON_(company)" title="ALISON (company)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwtA">Alison</a> – which are viewed as similar to MOOCs and work outside the university system or emphasize individual self-paced lessons.<sup about="#mwt77" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-CETIS78_24-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"CETIS78"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-CETIS78-24"}}'><a href="#cite_note-CETIS78-24" id="mwtQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwtg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtw">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt80" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Chronicle_25-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Chronicle"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Chronicle-25"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Chronicle-25" id="mwuQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwug"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuw">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt83" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-DavidBornstein_26-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"DavidBornstein"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-DavidBornstein-26"}}'><a href="#cite_note-DavidBornstein-26" id="mwvQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwvg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvw">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwwA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt87" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Information_Week_27-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Information Week"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Information_Week-27"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Information_Week-27" id="mwwQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwwg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwww">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt90" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYTIMES_28-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYTIMES"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYTIMES-28"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYTIMES-28" id="mwxQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxw">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyA">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwyQ" aria-labelledby="cMOOCs_and_xMOOCs"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="cMOOCs_and_xMOOCs">cMOOCs and xMOOCs</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=4" title="Edit section: cMOOCs and xMOOCs"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwyg"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Timeline_of_MOOC_and_open_education_development_with_organisational_efforts_in_the_areas.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwyw"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Timeline_of_MOOC_and_open_education_development_with_organisational_efforts_in_the_areas.png" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Timeline_of_MOOC_and_open_education_development_with_organisational_efforts_in_the_areas.png/500px-Timeline_of_MOOC_and_open_education_development_with_organisational_efforts_in_the_areas.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="866" data-file-height="564" data-file-type="bitmap" height="261" width="400" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/36/Timeline_of_MOOC_and_open_education_development_with_organisational_efforts_in_the_areas.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled 2x" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 1.6" id="mwzA"/></a><figcaption id="mwzQ">MOOCs and open-education timeline (updated 2015 version)<sup about="#mwt91" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-blogs.cetis.org.uk_12-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"blogs.cetis.org.uk"}}'><a href="#cite_note-blogs.cetis.org.uk-12" id="mwzg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwzw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0A">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0Q">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt94" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-29" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-29"}}'><a href="#cite_note-29" id="mw0g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw0w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1A">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1Q">]</span></span></a></sup></figcaption></figure> <p id="mw1g">As MOOCs developed with time, multiple conceptions of the platform seem to have emerged. Mostly two different types can be differentiated: those that emphasize a connectivist philosophy, and those that resemble more traditional courses. To distinguish the two, several early adopters of the platform proposed the terms "cMOOC" and "xMOOC".<sup about="#mwt97" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-30" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-30"}}'><a href="#cite_note-30" id="mw1w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw2A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2Q">[</span>30<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2g">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt100" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_31-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ReferenceA"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-ReferenceA-31"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-31" id="mw2w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw3A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3Q">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3g">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mw3w">cMOOCs are based on principles from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connectivism" title="Connectivism" id="mw4A">connectivist pedagogy</a> indicating that material should be <i id="mw4Q">aggregated</i> (rather than pre-selected), <i id="mw4g"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remix#Broader_context" title="Remix" id="mw4w">remixable</a></i>, <i id="mw5A">re-purposable</i>, and <i id="mw5Q">feeding forward</i> (i.e. evolving materials should be targeted at future learning).<sup about="#mwt102" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:11_32-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":11"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:11-32"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:11-32" id="mw5g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw5w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6A">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6Q">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt104" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:12_33-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":12"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:12-33"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:12-33" id="mw6g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw6w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7A">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7Q">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt106" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:13_34-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":13"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:13-34"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:13-34" id="mw7g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw7w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8A">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8Q">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt108" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:14_35-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":14"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:14-35"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:14-35" id="mw8g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw8w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9A">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9Q">]</span></span></a></sup> cMOOC instructional design approaches attempt to connect learners to each other to answer questions or collaborate on joint projects. This may include emphasizing collaborative development of the MOOC.<sup about="#mwt110" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:15_36-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":15"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:15-36"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:15-36" id="mw9g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw9w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-A">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-Q">]</span></span></a></sup> Andrew Ravenscroft of the London Metropolitan University claimed that connectivist MOOCs better support collaborative dialogue and knowledge building.<sup about="#mwt112" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Ravenscroft_37-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Ravenscroft"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Ravenscroft-37"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Ravenscroft-37" id="mw-g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw-w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_A">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_Q">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt114" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Mak_Williams_Mackness_38-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Mak Williams Mackness"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Mak_Williams_Mackness-38"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Mak_Williams_Mackness-38" id="mw_g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw_w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQA">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQE">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAQI">xMOOCs have a much more traditional course structure. They are characterized by a specified aim of completing the course obtaining certain knowledge certification of the subject matter. They are presented typically with a clearly specified syllabus of recorded lectures and self-test problems. However, some providers require paid subscriptions for acquiring graded materials and certificates. They employ elements of the original MOOC, but are, in some effect, branded IT platforms that offer content distribution partnerships to institutions.<sup about="#mwt115" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_31-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ReferenceA"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-31" id="mwAQM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQU">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQY">]</span></span></a></sup> The instructor is the expert provider of knowledge, and student interactions are usually limited to asking for assistance and advising each other on difficult points.</p> </section></section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwAQc" aria-labelledby="Equity_and_access"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Equity_and_access">Equity and access</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=5" title="Edit section: Equity and access"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwAQg">Access to Massive Open Online Courses (MOOCs) remains uneven across global populations, affecting individuals such as those with limited literacy and those residing in rural or economically disadvantaged regions. Prototypes for audio-based MOOCs have been created to allow those with limited literacy to use MOOCs.<sup about="#mwt118" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-39" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-39"}}'><a href="#cite_note-39" id="mwAQk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQs">[</span>39<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQw">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwAQ0" aria-labelledby="Emergence_of_MOOC_providers"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Emergence_of_MOOC_providers">Emergence of MOOC providers</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=6" title="Edit section: Emergence of MOOC providers"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAQ4"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gartner_Hype_Cycle.svg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAQ8"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gartner_Hype_Cycle.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Gartner_Hype_Cycle.svg/330px-Gartner_Hype_Cycle.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="559" data-file-height="363" data-file-type="drawing" height="195" width="300" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Gartner_Hype_Cycle.svg/960px-Gartner_Hype_Cycle.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 1.2" id="mwARA"/></a><figcaption id="mwARE">Dennis Yang, President of MOOC provider <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udemy" title="Udemy" id="mwARI">Udemy</a>, suggested in 2013 that MOOCs were in the midst of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hype_cycle" title="Hype cycle" class="mw-redirect" id="mwARM">hype cycle</a>, with expectations undergoing a wild swing.<sup about="#mwt121" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-MOOCd_out_40-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"MOOCd out"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-MOOCd_out-40"}}'><a href="#cite_note-MOOCd_out-40" id="mwARQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARY">[</span>40<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARc">]</span></span></a></sup> </figcaption></figure> <p id="mwARg">Since the 2010s, the industry has an unusual structure, consisting of linked groups including MOOC providers, the larger non-profit sector, universities, related companies and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capitalist" title="Venture capitalist" class="mw-redirect" id="mwARk">venture capitalists</a>. <i id="mwARo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Chronicle_of_Higher_Education" title="The Chronicle of Higher Education" id="mwARs">The Chronicle of Higher Education</a></i> lists the major providers as the non-profits Khan Academy and edX, and the for-profits Udacity and Coursera.<sup about="#mwt124" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Major_players_41-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Major players"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Major_players-41"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Major_players-41" id="mwARw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAR0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR4">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR8">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwASA">The larger non-profit organizations include the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_&_Melinda_Gates_Foundation" title="Bill & Melinda Gates Foundation" class="mw-redirect" id="mwASE">Bill & Melinda Gates Foundation</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MacArthur_Foundation" title="MacArthur Foundation" id="mwASI">MacArthur Foundation</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Science_Foundation" title="National Science Foundation" id="mwASM">National Science Foundation</a>, and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Council_on_Education" title="American Council on Education" id="mwASQ">American Council on Education</a>. University pioneers include <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford" title="Stanford" class="mw-redirect" id="mwASU">Stanford</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard" title="Harvard" class="mw-redirect" id="mwASY">Harvard</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Institute_of_Technology" title="Massachusetts Institute of Technology" id="mwASc">MIT</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania" title="University of Pennsylvania" id="mwASg">University of Pennsylvania</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Institute_of_Technology" title="California Institute of Technology" id="mwASk">Caltech</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Texas_at_Austin" title="University of Texas at Austin" id="mwASo">University of Texas at Austin</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California_at_Berkeley" title="University of California at Berkeley" class="mw-redirect" id="mwASs">University of California at Berkeley</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose_State_University" title="San Jose State University" id="mwASw">San Jose State University</a>.<sup about="#mwt125" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Major_players_41-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Major players"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Major_players-41" id="mwAS0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAS4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS8">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATA">]</span></span></a></sup> Related companies investing in MOOCs include <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google" title="Google" id="mwATE">Google</a> and educational publisher <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearson_PLC" title="Pearson PLC" class="mw-redirect" id="mwATI">Pearson PLC</a>. Venture capitalists include <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kleiner_Perkins_Caufield_&_Byers" title="Kleiner Perkins Caufield & Byers" class="mw-redirect" id="mwATM">Kleiner Perkins Caufield & Byers</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Enterprise_Associates" title="New Enterprise Associates" id="mwATQ">New Enterprise Associates</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andreessen_Horowitz" title="Andreessen Horowitz" id="mwATU">Andreessen Horowitz</a>.<sup about="#mwt126" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Major_players_41-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Major players"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Major_players-41" id="mwATY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATg">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATk">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwATo">In the fall of 2011, Stanford University launched three courses.<sup about="#mwt129" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT71712_42-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT71712"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYT71712-42"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT71712-42" id="mwATs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAT0">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAT4">]</span></span></a></sup> The first of those courses was <i id="mwAT8">Introduction Into AI</i>, launched by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Thrun" title="Sebastian Thrun" id="mwAUA">Sebastian Thrun</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Norvig" title="Peter Norvig" id="mwAUE">Peter Norvig</a>. Enrollment quickly reached 160,000 students. The announcement was followed within weeks by the launch of two more MOOCs, by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Ng" title="Andrew Ng" id="mwAUI">Andrew Ng</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Widom" title="Jennifer Widom" id="mwAUM">Jennifer Widom</a>. Following the publicity and high enrollment numbers of these courses, Thrun started a company he named Udacity and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daphne_Koller" title="Daphne Koller" id="mwAUQ">Daphne Koller</a> and Andrew Ng launched Coursera.<sup about="#mwt132" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-43" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-43"}}'><a href="#cite_note-43" id="mwAUU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUc">[</span>43<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUg">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAUk">In January 2013, Udacity launched its first MOOCs-for-credit, in collaboration with San Jose State University. In May 2013, the company announced the first entirely MOOC-based master's degree, a collaboration between Udacity, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT&T" title="AT&T" id="mwAUo">AT&T</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_Institute_of_Technology" title="Georgia Institute of Technology" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAUs">Georgia Institute of Technology</a>, costing $7,000, a fraction of its normal tuition.<sup about="#mwt135" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-44" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-44"}}'><a href="#cite_note-44" id="mwAUw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAU0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU4">[</span>44<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU8">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAVA">Concerned about the commercialization of online education, in 2012 MIT created the not-for-profit MITx.<sup about="#mwt138" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-45" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-45"}}'><a href="#cite_note-45" id="mwAVE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVM">[</span>45<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVQ">]</span></span></a></sup> The inaugural course, 6.002x, launched in March 2012. Harvard joined the group, renamed edX, that spring, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley" title="University of California, Berkeley" id="mwAVU">University of California, Berkeley</a> joined in the summer. The initiative then added the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Texas_System" title="University of Texas System" id="mwAVY">University of Texas System</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellesley_College" title="Wellesley College" id="mwAVc">Wellesley College</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgetown_University" title="Georgetown University" id="mwAVg">Georgetown University</a>.</p> <p id="mwAVk">In September 2013, edX announced a partnership with Google to develop MOOC.org, a site for non-xConsortium groups to build and host courses. Google will work on the core platform development with edX partners. In addition, Google and edX will collaborate on research into how students learn and how technology can transform learning and teaching. MOOC.org will adopt Google's infrastructure.<sup about="#mwt141" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-46" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-46"}}'><a href="#cite_note-46" id="mwAVo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVw">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAV0">]</span></span></a></sup> The Chinese <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsinghua_University" title="Tsinghua University" id="mwAV4">Tsinghua University</a> MOOC platform XuetangX.com (launched Oct. 2013) uses the Open edX platform.<sup about="#mwt144" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:18_47-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":18"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:18-47"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:18-47" id="mwAV8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWE">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWI">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAWM">Before 2013, each MOOC tended to develop its own delivery platform. EdX in April 2013 joined with Stanford University, which previously had its own platform called Class2Go, to work on <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/XBlock" title="XBlock" id="mwAWQ">XBlock</a> SDK, a joint open-source platform. It is available to the public under the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNU_Affero_General_Public_License" title="GNU Affero General Public License" id="mwAWU">AGPL</a> open source license, which requires that all improvements to the platform be publicly posted and made available under the same license.<sup about="#mwt148" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-edXPressRelease_48-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"edXPressRelease"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-edXPressRelease-48"}}'><a href="#cite_note-edXPressRelease-48" id="mwAWY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWg">[</span>48<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWk">]</span></span></a></sup> Stanford Vice Provost John Mitchell said that the goal was to provide the "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linux" title="Linux" id="mwAWo">Linux</a> of online learning".<sup about="#mwt151" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Open_Source_49-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Open Source"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Open_Source-49"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Open_Source-49" id="mwAWs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAW0">[</span>49<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAW4">]</span></span></a></sup> This is unlike companies such as Coursera that have developed their own platform.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt145" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAW8" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cn","href":"./Template:Cn"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"May 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_May_2026" about="#mwt145"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt145" id="mwAXA"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2026)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p> <p id="mwAXE">By November 2013, edX offered 94 courses from 29 institutions around the world. During its first 13 months of operation (ending March 2013), Coursera offered about 325 courses, with 30% in the sciences, 28% in arts and humanities, 23% in information technology, 13% in business and 6% in mathematics.<sup about="#mwt152" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-SA31313_50-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"SA31313"}}'><a href="#cite_note-SA31313-50" id="mwAXI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXQ">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXU">]</span></span></a></sup> Udacity offered 26 courses. The number of courses offered has since increased dramatically: As of January 2016, edx offers 820 courses, Coursera offers 1580 courses and Udacity offers more than 120 courses. According to FutureLearn, the British Council's Understanding IELTS: Techniques for English Language Tests has an enrollment of over 440,000 students.<sup about="#mwt155" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:19_51-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":19"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:19-51"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:19-51" id="mwAXY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXg">[</span>51<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXk">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwAXo" aria-labelledby="Notable_providers"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Notable_providers">Notable providers</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=7" title="Edit section: Notable providers"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt156" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAXs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"List of MOOC providers"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1353705441" about="#mwt157" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt156" id="mwAXw">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_MOOC_providers" title="List of MOOC providers">List of MOOC providers</a></div> </section><section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwAX0" aria-labelledby="Emergence_of_innovative_courses"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Emergence_of_innovative_courses">Emergence of innovative courses</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=8" title="Edit section: Emergence of innovative courses"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwAX4">Early cMOOCs such as CCK08 and ds106 used innovative pedagogy (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connectivism" title="Connectivism" id="mwAX8">Connectivism</a>), with distributed learning materials rather than a video-lecture format, and a focus on education and learning, and digital storytelling respectively<sup about="#mwt158" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-THE_18-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"THE"}}'><a href="#cite_note-THE-18" id="mwAYA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYI">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt159" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-downes_19-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"downes"}}'><a href="#cite_note-downes-19" id="mwAYQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYY">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYc">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt160" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-auto_20-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"auto"}}'><a href="#cite_note-auto-20" id="mwAYg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYo">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYs">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt161" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-auto1_21-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"auto1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-auto1-21" id="mwAYw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAY0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY4">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt162" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-GROOM_22-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"GROOM"}}'><a href="#cite_note-GROOM-22" id="mwAZA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZI">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZM">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAZQ">Following the 2011 launch of three Stanford xMOOCs, including <i id="mwAZU">Introduction Into AI</i>, launched by Sebastian Thrun and Peter Norvig<sup about="#mwt163" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT71712_42-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT71712"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT71712-42" id="mwAZY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZg">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZk">]</span></span></a></sup> a number of other innovative courses have emerged. As of May 2014, more than 900 MOOCs are offered by US universities and colleges. As of February 2013, dozens of universities had affiliated with MOOCs, including many international institutions.<sup about="#mwt166" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT022013_52-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT022013"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYT022013-52"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT022013-52" id="mwAZo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZw">[</span>52<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZ0">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt169" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-CHE022113_53-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"CHE022113"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-CHE022113-53"}}'><a href="#cite_note-CHE022113-53" id="mwAZ4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZ8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaA">[</span>53<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaE">]</span></span></a></sup> In addition, some organisations operate their own MOOCs – including Google's Power Search.</p> <p id="mwAaI">A range of courses have emerged; "There was a real question of whether this would work for humanities and social science", said Ng. However, psychology and philosophy courses are among Coursera's most popular. Student feedback and completion rates suggest that they are as successful as math and science courses<sup about="#mwt172" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-wsj1013_54-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"wsj1013"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-wsj1013-54"}}'><a href="#cite_note-wsj1013-54" id="mwAaM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaU">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaY">]</span></span></a></sup> even though the corresponding completion rates are lower.<sup about="#mwt173" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-in_AIS_Electronic_Library_AISeL_9-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"in AIS Electronic Library AISeL"}}'><a href="#cite_note-in_AIS_Electronic_Library_AISeL-9" id="mwAac"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAag"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAak">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAao">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAas">In January 2012, University of Helsinki launched a Finnish MOOC in programming. The MOOC is used as a way to offer high-schools the opportunity to provide programming courses for their students, even if no local premises or faculty that can organize such courses exist.<sup about="#mwt176" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-VihavainenMOOC2012_55-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"VihavainenMOOC2012"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-VihavainenMOOC2012-55"}}'><a href="#cite_note-VihavainenMOOC2012-55" id="mwAaw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAa0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa4">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa8">]</span></span></a></sup> The course has been offered recurringly, and the top-performing students are admitted to a BSc and MSc program in Computer Science at the University of Helsinki.<sup about="#mwt177" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-VihavainenMOOC2012_55-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"VihavainenMOOC2012"}}'><a href="#cite_note-VihavainenMOOC2012-55" id="mwAbA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbI">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt180" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:20_56-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":20"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:20-56"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:20-56" id="mwAbQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbY">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbc">]</span></span></a></sup> At a meeting on E-Learning and MOOCs, Jaakko Kurhila, Head of studies for University of Helsinki, Department of Computer Science, claimed that to date, there have been over 8000 participants in their MOOCs altogether.<sup about="#mwt183" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:21_57-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":21"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:21-57"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:21-57" id="mwAbg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbo">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbs">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAbw">On June 18, 2012, Ali Lemus from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galileo_University" title="Galileo University" id="mwAb0">Galileo University</a><sup about="#mwt186" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:22_58-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":22"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:22-58"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:22-58" id="mwAb4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAb8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcA">[</span>58<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcE">]</span></span></a></sup> launched the first Latin American MOOC titled "Desarrollando Aplicaciones para iPhone y iPad"<sup about="#mwt189" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:23_59-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":23"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:23-59"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:23-59" id="mwAcI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcQ">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcU">]</span></span></a></sup> This MOOC is a Spanish remix of Stanford University's popular "CS 193P iPhone Application Development" and had 5,380 students enrolled. The technology used to host the MOOC was the Galileo Educational System platform (GES) which is based on the .LRN project.<sup about="#mwt192" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:24_60-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":24"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:24-60"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:24-60" id="mwAcY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcg">[</span>60<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAck">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAco">"Gender Through Comic Books" was a course taught by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ball_State_University" title="Ball State University" id="mwAcs">Ball State University</a>'s Christina Blanch on Instructure's Canvas Network, a MOOC platform launched in November 2012.<sup about="#mwt195" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-comicbook_61-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"comicbook"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-comicbook-61"}}'><a href="#cite_note-comicbook-61" id="mwAcw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAc0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAc4">[</span>61<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAc8">]</span></span></a></sup> The course used examples from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comic_books" title="Comic books" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAdA">comic books</a> to teach academic concepts about gender and perceptions.<sup about="#mwt198" Courses were originally delivered by Moodle, but were looking more like other MOOCs by 2012.<sup about="#mwt274" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-88" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-88"}}'><a href="#cite_note-88" id="mwAmU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAmY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmc">[</span>88<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmg">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section></section><section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwAmk" aria-labelledby="Instructor_role_and_quality_assurance"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Instructor_role_and_quality_assurance">Instructor role and quality assurance</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=9" title="Edit section: Instructor role and quality assurance"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwAmo">The role of instructors in evaluating and shaping the quality of MOOCs has emerged as a significant concern, particularly within competency-based and open learning contexts.</p> <p id="mwAms">Although teachers acknowledge the importance of widely endorsed principles including learner autonomy, accessibility, and flexibility, their assessments tend to prioritize practical teaching experience and learner engagement over formalized evaluation frameworks. Participants highlighted the value of adaptive course structures, embedded support mechanisms, and interactive content as indicators of quality. However, there was limited awareness of or alignment with institutional quality assurance protocols.<sup about="#mwt275" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:14_35-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:14-35" id="mwAmw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAm0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAm4">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAm8">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAnA">This study contributes to a broader understanding of instructional design and quality assurance in MOOCs by emphasizing the disconnect between top-down standards and bottom-up pedagogical practices. It suggests the need for a more integrated approach that incorporates instructors’ experiential knowledge into formal evaluation systems to enhance course effectiveness and instructional quality (Chang & Sun, 2025).<sup about="#mwt276" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:15_36-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:15-36" id="mwAnE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnM">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwAnU" aria-labelledby="Student_experience_and_pedagogy"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Student_experience_and_pedagogy">Student experience and pedagogy</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=10" title="Edit section: Student experience and pedagogy"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwAnY">The learner base for MOOCs has grown substantially over the past decade, reflecting increasing global interest in flexible, online education. As of May 2025, Coursera, one of the largest MOOC platforms, reported more than 148 million registered learners worldwide, signifying a major expansion from its earlier count of 5 million users in 2013 (Infostride, 2025).<sup about="#mwt278" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:16_89-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":16"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:16-89"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:16-89" id="mwAnc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAng"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnk">[</span>89<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAno">]</span></span></a></sup> This growth is attributed to factors such as the broadening of course offerings, the incorporation of micro-credentials, and partnerships with universities and industries. The evolving student experience increasingly emphasizes interactive content, real-world application, and personalized learning pathways, aligning with contemporary pedagogical practices in digital education.<sup about="#mwt281" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:17_90-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":17"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:17-90"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:17-90" id="mwAns"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn0">[</span>90<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn4">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAn8">By June 2012, more than 1.5 million people had registered for classes through Coursera, Udacity or edX. As of 2013, the range of students registered appears to be broad, diverse and non-traditional, but concentrated among English-speakers in rich countries. By March 2013, Coursera alone had registered about 2.8 million learners. By October 2013, Coursera enrollment continued to surge, surpassing 5 million, while edX had independently reached 1.3 million.</p> <p id="mwAoA">A course billed as "Asia's first MOOC" given by the Hong Kong University of Science and Technology through Coursera starting in April 2013 registered 17,000 students. About 60% were from "rich countries" with many of the rest from middle-income countries in Asia, South Africa, Brazil or Mexico. Fewer students enrolled from areas with more limited access to the internet, and students from the People's Republic of China may have been discouraged by Chinese government policies. Koller stated in May 2013 that a majority of the people taking Coursera courses had already earned college degrees.</p> <p id="mwAoE">According to a Stanford University study of a more general group of students—"active learners," defined as those who participated beyond merely registering—64% of high school active learners were male, and 88% were male for undergraduate- and graduate-level courses. A study from Stanford University's Learning Analytics group identified four types of students: auditors, who watched video throughout the course but took few quizzes or exams; completers, who viewed most lectures and took part in most assessments; disengaged learners, who quickly dropped the course; and sampling learners, who might only occasionally watch lectures. They identified the following percentages in each group:</p> <table class="wikitable" id="mwAoI"> <tbody id="mwAoM"><tr id="mwAoQ"><th id="mwAoU">Course</th> <th id="mwAoY">Auditing</th> <th id="mwAoc">Completing</th> <th id="mwAog">Disengaging</th> <th id="mwAok">Sampling</th></tr> <tr id="mwAoo"> <td id="mwAos">High school</td> <td id="mwAow">6%</td> <td id="mwAo0">27%</td> <td id="mwAo4">29%</td> <td id="mwAo8">39%</td></tr> <tr id="mwApA"> <td id="mwApE">Undergraduate</td> <td id="mwApI">6%</td> <td id="mwApM">8%</td> <td id="mwApQ">12%</td> <td id="mwApU">74%</td></tr> <tr id="mwApY"> <td id="mwApc">Graduate</td> <td id="mwApg">9%</td> <td id="mwApk">5%</td> <td id="mwApo">6%</td> <td id="mwAps">80%</td></tr> </tbody></table> <p id="mwApw">Jonathan Haber focused on questions of what students are learning and student demographics. About half the students taking U.S. courses are from other countries and do not speak English as their first language. He found some courses to be meaningful, especially those addressing reading comprehension. Video lectures followed by multiple-choice questions can be challenging since they often ask the "right questions." Smaller discussion boards paradoxically offer the best conversations. Larger discussions can be "really, really thoughtful and really, really misguided," with long threads sometimes devolving into rehashes or "the same old stale left/right debate."</p> <p id="mwAp0">MIT and Stanford University offered initial MOOCs in Computer Science and Electrical Engineering. Since engineering courses require prerequisites, upper-level engineering courses were initially absent from MOOC platforms. However, by 2015, several universities were offering both undergraduate and advanced-level engineering courses.</p> <section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwAp4" aria-labelledby="Educator_experience"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Educator_experience">Educator experience</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=11" title="Edit section: Educator experience"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwAp8">In 2013, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chronicle_of_Higher_Education" title="Chronicle of Higher Education" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAqA">Chronicle of Higher Education</a> surveyed 103 professors who had taught MOOCs. "Typically a professor spent over 100 hours on his MOOC before it even started, by recording online lecture videos and doing other preparation", though some instructors' pre-class preparation was "a few dozen hours". The professors then spent 8–10 hours per week on the course, including participation in discussion forums.<sup about="#mwt282" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Chron_survey_91-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Chron survey"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Chron_survey-91" id="mwAqE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqM">[</span>91<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAqU">The medians were: 33,000 students enrollees; 2,600 passing; and 1 teaching assistant helping with the class. 74% of the classes used automated grading, and 34% used peer grading. 97% of the instructors used original videos, 75% used open educational resources and 27% used other resources. 9% of the classes required a physical textbook and 5% required an e-book.<sup about="#mwt285" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Chron_survey_91-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Chron survey"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Chron_survey-91"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Chron_survey-91" id="mwAqY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqg">[</span>91<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqk">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt288" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-additional_results_92-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"additional results"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-additional_results-92"}}'><a href="#cite_note-additional_results-92" id="mwAqo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqw">[</span>92<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAq0">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAq4">Unlike traditional courses, MOOCs require additional skills, provided by videographers, instructional designers, IT specialists and platform specialists. Georgia Tech professor <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Head" title="Karen Head" id="mwAq8">Karen Head</a> reports that 19 people work on their MOOCs and that more are needed.<sup about="#mwt291" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-GTech_93-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"GTech"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-GTech-93"}}'><a href="#cite_note-GTech-93" id="mwArA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArI">[</span>93<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArM">]</span></span></a></sup> The platforms have availability requirements similar to media/content sharing websites, due to the large number of enrollees. MOOCs typically use <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloud_computing" title="Cloud computing" id="mwArQ">cloud computing</a> and are often created with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoring_system" title="Authoring system" id="mwArU">authoring systems</a>. Authoring tools for the creation of MOOCs are specialized packages of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Educational_software" title="Educational software" id="mwArY">educational software</a> like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elicitus" title="Elicitus" id="mwArc">Elicitus</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMC_Content_Studio" title="IMC Content Studio" id="mwArg">IMC Content Studio</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lectora?action=edit&redlink=1" title="Lectora (page does not exist)" class="new" typeof="mw:LocalizedAttrs" id="mwArk" data-mw-i18n='{"title":{"lang":"x-page","key":"red-link-title","params":["Lectora"]}}'>Lectora</a> that are easy-to-use and support e-learning standards like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCORM" title="SCORM" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAro">SCORM</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aviation_Industry_Computer-Based_Training_Committee" title="Aviation Industry Computer-Based Training Committee" id="mwArs">AICC</a>.</p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwArw" aria-labelledby="Instructional_design"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Instructional_design">Instructional design</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=12" title="Edit section: Instructional design"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt292" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAr0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"External media ","href":"./Template:External_media"},"params":{"width":{"wt":"210px"},"float":{"wt":"right"},"headerimage":{"wt":"[[File:Duke Chapel
