class="mw-file-element"/></a></span> <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JKbPNx2TSgM" class="external text"><span class="plainlinks">10 Steps to Developing an Online Course: Walter Sinnott-Armstrong</span></a> on <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTube_video_(identifier)" title="YouTube video (identifier)" class="mw-redirect">YouTube</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_University" title="Duke University">Duke University</a><sup about="#mwt295" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Duke_U_94-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Duke U"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Duke_U-94"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Duke_U-94" id="mwAr8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsE">[</span>94<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsI">]</span></span></a></sup></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data" style="text-align: left"><span class="noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="video icon" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg/20px-Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="128" data-file-height="128" data-file-type="drawing" height="16" width="16" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg/40px-Nuvola_apps_kaboodle.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element"/></a></span> <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://www.slideshare.net/gsiemens/designing-and-running-a-mooc" class="external text" data-mw-original-href="http://www.slideshare.net/gsiemens/designing-and-running-a-mooc">Designing, developing and running (Massive) Online Courses</a> by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Siemens" title="George Siemens">George Siemens</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabasca_University" title="Athabasca University">Athabasca University</a><sup about="#mwt299" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Atha_U_95-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Atha U"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Atha_U-95"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Atha_U-95" id="mwAsM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsU">[</span>95<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsY">]</span></span></a></sup></td></tr></tbody></table> <p id="mwAsc">Many MOOCs use video <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lecture" title="Lecture" id="mwAsg">lectures</a>, employing the old form of teaching (lecturing) using a new technology.<sup about="#mwt304" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Yousef_96-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Yousef"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Yousef-96"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Yousef-96" id="mwAsk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAso"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAss">[</span>96<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt307" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-EReality_97-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"EReality"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-EReality-97"}}'><a href="#cite_note-EReality-97" id="mwAs0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAs4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAs8">[</span>97<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtA">]</span></span></a></sup> Thrun testified before the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President’s_Council_of_Advisors_on_Science_and_Technology" title="President’s Council of Advisors on Science and Technology" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAtE">President’s Council of Advisors on Science and Technology</a> (PCAST) that MOOC "courses are 'designed to be challenges,' not lectures, and the amount of data generated from these assessments can be evaluated 'massively using machine learning' at work behind the scenes. This approach, he said, dispels 'the medieval set of myths' guiding teacher efficacy and student outcomes, and replaces it with evidence-based, 'modern, data-driven' educational methodologies that may be the instruments responsible for a 'fundamental transformation of education' itself".<sup about="#mwt310" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Librarians_and_the_Era_of_the_MOOC_98-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Librarians and the Era of the MOOC"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Librarians_and_the_Era_of_the_MOOC-98"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Librarians_and_the_Era_of_the_MOOC-98" id="mwAtI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtQ">[</span>98<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtU">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAtY">Some view the videos and other material produced by the MOOC as the next form of the textbook. "MOOC is the new textbook", according to David Finegold of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_University" title="Rutgers University" id="mwAtc">Rutgers University</a>.<sup about="#mwt313" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-99" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-99"}}'><a href="#cite_note-99" id="mwAtg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAto">[</span>99<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAts">]</span></span></a></sup> A study of edX student habits found that certificate-earning students generally stop watching videos longer than 6 to 9 minutes. They viewed the first 4.4 minutes (median) of 12- to 15-minute videos.<sup about="#mwt316" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-100" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-100"}}'><a href="#cite_note-100" id="mwAtw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAt0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAt4">[</span>100<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAt8">]</span></span></a></sup> Some traditional schools blend online and offline learning, sometimes called flipped classrooms. Students watch lectures online at home and work on projects and interact with faculty while in class. Such hybrids can even improve student performance in traditional in-person classes. One fall 2012 test by San Jose State and edX found that incorporating content from an online course into a for-credit campus-based course increased pass rates to 91% from as low as 55% without the online component. "We do not recommend selecting an online-only experience over a blended learning experience", says Coursera's Andrew Ng.<sup about="#mwt317" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-wsj1013_54-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"wsj1013"}}'><a href="#cite_note-wsj1013-54" id="mwAuA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAuE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuI">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuM">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAuQ">Because of massive enrollments, MOOCs require instructional design that facilitates large-scale feedback and interaction. The two basic approaches are:</p> <ul id="mwAuU"><li id="mwAuY">Peer-review and group collaboration</li> <li id="mwAuc">Automated feedback through objective, online assessments, e.g. quizzes and exams<sup about="#mwt318" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-DoF_1_101-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"DoF 1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-DoF_1-101" id="mwAug"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAuk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuo">[</span>101<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAus">]</span></span></a></sup> Machine grading of written assignments is also underway.<sup about="#mwt321" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-IHE_1_102-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"IHE 1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-IHE_1-102"}}'><a href="#cite_note-IHE_1-102" id="mwAuw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAu0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAu4">[</span>102<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAu8">]</span></span></a></sup></li></ul> <p id="mwAvA">So-called connectivist MOOCs rely on the former approach; broadcast MOOCs rely more on the latter.<sup about="#mwt323" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-103" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-103"}}'><a href="#cite_note-103" id="mwAvE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvM">[</span>103<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvQ">]</span></span></a></sup> This marks a key distinction between <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Connectivist_design" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwAvU">cMOOCs</a> where the 'C' stands for 'connectivist', and xMOOCs where the x stands for extended (as in TEDx, edX) and represents that the MOOC is designed to be in addition to something else (university courses for example).<sup about="#mwt326" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-xMOOC_def_104-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"xMOOC def"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-xMOOC_def-104"}}'><a href="#cite_note-xMOOC_def-104" id="mwAvY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvg">[</span>104<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvk">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAvo">Assessment can be the most difficult activity to conduct online, and online assessments can be quite different from the brick-and-mortar version.<sup about="#mwt328" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-DoF_1_101-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"DoF 1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-DoF_1-101"}}'><a href="#cite_note-DoF_1-101" id="mwAvs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAv0">[</span>101<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAv4">]</span></span></a></sup> Special attention has been devoted to proctoring and cheating.<sup about="#mwt331" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT030212_105-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT030212"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYT030212-105"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT030212-105" id="mwAv8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwE">[</span>105<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwI">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAwM">Peer review is often based upon sample answers or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubric_(academic)" title="Rubric (academic)" id="mwAwQ">rubrics</a>, which guide the grader on how many points to award different answers. These rubrics cannot be as complex for peer grading as for teaching assistants. Students are expected to learn via grading others<sup about="#mwt334" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-106" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-106"}}'><a href="#cite_note-106" id="mwAwU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwc">[</span>106<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwg">]</span></span></a></sup> and become more engaged with the course.<sup about="#mwt335" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-in_AIS_Electronic_Library_AISeL_9-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"in AIS Electronic Library AISeL"}}'><a href="#cite_note-in_AIS_Electronic_Library_AISeL-9" id="mwAwk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAws">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAww">]</span></span></a></sup> Exams may be proctored at regional testing centers. Other methods, including "eavesdropping technologies worthy of the C.I.A.", allow testing at home or office, by using webcams, or monitoring mouse clicks and typing styles.<sup about="#mwt336" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT030212_105-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT030212"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT030212-105" id="mwAw0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAw4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAw8">[</span>105<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxA">]</span></span></a></sup> Special techniques such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computerized_adaptive_testing" title="Computerized adaptive testing" id="mwAxE">adaptive testing</a> may be used, where the test tailors itself given the student's previous answers, giving harder or easier questions accordingly.</p> <p id="mwAxI">"The most important thing that helps students succeed in an online course is interpersonal interaction and support", says Shanna Smith Jaggars, assistant director of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University" title="Columbia University" id="mwAxM">Columbia University</a>'s <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_College_Research_Center" title="Community College Research Center" id="mwAxQ">Community College Research Center</a>. Her research compared online-only and face-to-face learning in studies of community-college students and faculty in Virginia and Washington state. Among her findings: In Virginia, 32% of students failed or withdrew from for-credit online courses, compared with 19% for equivalent in-person courses.<sup about="#mwt337" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-wsj1013_54-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"wsj1013"}}'><a href="#cite_note-wsj1013-54" id="mwAxU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAxY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxc">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxg">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAxk">Assigning mentors to students is another interaction-enhancing technique.<sup about="#mwt338" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-wsj1013_54-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"wsj1013"}}'><a href="#cite_note-wsj1013-54" id="mwAxo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAxs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxw">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAx0">]</span></span></a></sup> In 2013 Harvard offered a popular class, <i id="mwAx4">The Ancient Greek Hero</i>, instructed by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregory_Nagy" title="Gregory Nagy" id="mwAx8">Gregory Nagy</a> and taken by thousands of Harvard students over prior decades. It appealed to alumni to volunteer as online mentors and discussion group managers. About 10 former teaching fellows also volunteered. The task of the volunteers, which required 3–5 hours per week, was to focus on online class discussion. The edX course registered 27,000 students.<sup about="#mwt341" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT032513_107-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT032513"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYT032513-107"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT032513-107" id="mwAyA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAyE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyI">[</span>107<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyM">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAyQ">Research by Kop and Fournier<sup about="#mwt344" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-oltraining6_108-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"oltraining6"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-oltraining6-108"}}'><a href="#cite_note-oltraining6-108" id="mwAyU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAyY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyc">[</span>108<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyg">]</span></span></a></sup> highlighted as major challenges the lack of social presence and the high level of autonomy required. Techniques for maintaining connection with students include adding audio comments on assignments instead of writing them, participating with students in the discussion forums, asking brief questions in the middle of the lecture, updating weekly videos about the course and sending congratulatory emails on prior accomplishments to students who are slightly behind.<sup about="#mwt345" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-wsj1013_54-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"wsj1013"}}'><a href="#cite_note-wsj1013-54" id="mwAyk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAyo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAys">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyw">]</span></span></a></sup> Grading by peer review has had mixed results. In one example, three fellow students grade one assignment for each assignment that they submit. The grading key or rubric tends to focus the grading, but discourages more creative writing.<sup about="#mwt348" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-BG_Haber_109-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"BG Haber"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-BG_Haber-109"}}'><a href="#cite_note-BG_Haber-109" id="mwAy0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAy4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAy8">[</span>109<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzA">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwAzE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._J._Jacobs" title="A. J. Jacobs" id="mwAzI">A. J. Jacobs</a> in an op-ed in <i id="mwAzM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times" id="mwAzQ">The New York Times</a></i> graded his experience in 11 MOOC classes overall as a "B".<sup about="#mwt351" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-AJJ_110-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"AJJ"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-AJJ-110"}}'><a href="#cite_note-AJJ-110" id="mwAzU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzc">[</span>110<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzg">]</span></span></a></sup> He rated his professors as '"B+", despite "a couple of clunkers", even comparing them to pop stars and "A-list celebrity professors". Nevertheless, he rated teacher-to-student interaction as a "D" since he had almost no contact with the professors. The highest-rated ("A") aspect of Jacobs' experience was the ability to watch videos at any time. Student-to-student interaction and assignments both received "B−". Study groups that did not meet, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troll_(Internet)" title="Troll (Internet)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAzk">trolls</a> on message boards and the relative slowness of online vs. personal conversations lowered that rating. Assignments included multiple-choice quizzes and exams as well as essays and projects. He found the multiple-choice tests stressful and peer-graded essays painful. He completed only 2 of the 11 classes.<sup about="#mwt352" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-AJJ_110-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"AJJ"}}'><a href="#cite_note-AJJ-110" id="mwAzo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzw">[</span>110<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAz0">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt355" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NPR_AJ_111-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NPR AJ"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NPR_AJ-111"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NPR_AJ-111" id="mwAz4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAz8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0A">[</span>111<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0E">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section></section><section data-mw-section-id="13" id="mwA0I" aria-labelledby="Industry"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Industry">Industry</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=13" title="Edit section: Industry"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwA0M">MOOCs are widely seen as a major part of a larger <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disruptive_innovation" title="Disruptive innovation" id="mwA0Q">disruptive innovation</a> taking place in higher education.<sup about="#mwt358" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-avalanche_112-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"avalanche"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-avalanche-112"}}'><a href="#cite_note-avalanche-112" id="mwA0U"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0Y"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0c">[</span>112<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0g">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt361" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Times_HE_113-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Times HE"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Times_HE-113"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Times_HE-113" id="mwA0k"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0o"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0s">[</span>113<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0w">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt364" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-114" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-114"}}'><a href="#cite_note-114" id="mwA00"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA04"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA08">[</span>114<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1A">]</span></span></a></sup> In particular, the many services offered under traditional university business models are predicted to become <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unbundling" title="Unbundling" id="mwA1E">unbundled</a> and sold to students individually or in newly formed bundles.<sup about="#mwt367" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-KCarey_115-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"KCarey"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-KCarey-115"}}'><a href="#cite_note-KCarey-115" id="mwA1I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1Q">[</span>115<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1U">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt370" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NHarden_116-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NHarden"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NHarden-116"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NHarden-116" id="mwA1Y"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1c"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1g">[</span>116<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1k">]</span></span></a></sup> These services include research, curriculum design, content generation (such as textbooks), teaching, assessment and certification (such as granting degrees) and student placement. MOOCs threaten existing business models by potentially selling teaching, assessment, or placement separately from the current package of services.<sup about="#mwt371" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-avalanche_112-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"avalanche"}}'><a href="#cite_note-avalanche-112" id="mwA1o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1w">[</span>112<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA10">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt374" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Zhu_117-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Zhu"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Zhu-117"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Zhu-117" id="mwA14"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA18"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2A">[</span>117<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2E">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt377" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Shirky_118-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Shirky"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Shirky-118"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Shirky-118" id="mwA2I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2Q">[</span>118<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2U">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwA2Y">Former President <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama" title="Barack Obama" id="mwA2c">Barack Obama</a> cited recent developments, including the online learning innovations at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnegie_Mellon_University" title="Carnegie Mellon University" id="mwA2g">Carnegie Mellon University</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arizona_State_University" title="Arizona State University" id="mwA2k">Arizona State University</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_Tech" title="Georgia Tech" id="mwA2o">Georgia Institute of Technology</a>, as having potential to reduce the rising costs of higher education.<sup about="#mwt380" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-WhiteHouse_119-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"WhiteHouse"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-WhiteHouse-119"}}'><a href="#cite_note-WhiteHouse-119" id="mwA2s"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA20">[</span>119<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA24">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwA28">James Mazoue, Director of Online Programs at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_State_University" title="Wayne State University" id="mwA3A">Wayne State University</a> describes one possible innovation:</p> <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1244412712" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt381" id="mwA3E" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Blockquote/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Quote","href":"./Template:Quote"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"The next disruptor will likely mark a tipping point: an entirely free online curriculum leading to a degree from an accredited institution. With this new business model, students might still have to pay to certify their credentials, but not for the process leading to their acquisition. If free access to a degree-granting curriculum were to occur, the business model of higher education would dramatically and irreversibly change.<ref name=Mazoue>{{cite web|last=Mazoue|first=James G.|title=The MOOC Model: Challenging Traditional Education|url=http://www.educause.edu/ero/article/mooc-model-challenging-traditional-education|publisher=EDUCAUSE Review Online|access-date=26 March 2013|date=28 January 2013|archive-date=31 March 2013|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130331061721/http://www.educause.edu/ero/article/mooc-model-challenging-traditional-education|url-status=live}}</ref>"}},"i":0}}]}'>.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 32px}.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;margin-top:0}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{padding-left:1.6em}}</style><blockquote class="templatequote " about="#mwt381" id="mwA3I"><p>The next disruptor will likely mark a tipping point: an entirely free online curriculum leading to a degree from an accredited institution. With this new business model, students might still have to pay to certify their credentials, but not for the process leading to their acquisition. If free access to a degree-granting curriculum were to occur, the business model of higher education would dramatically and irreversibly change.<sup about="#mwt385" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Mazoue_120-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Mazoue"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Mazoue-120"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Mazoue-120" id="mwA3M"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA3Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3U">[</span>120<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3Y">]</span></span></a></sup></p></blockquote> <p id="mwA3c">But how universities will benefit by "giving our product away free online" is unclear.<sup about="#mwt388" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT010613_121-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT010613"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT010613-121" id="mwA3g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA3k"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3o">[</span>121<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA3s">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1244412712" about="#mwt389" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3w" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Blockquote/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Quote","href":"./Template:Quote"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"No one's got the model that's going to work yet. I expect all the current ventures to fail, because the expectations are too high. People think something will catch on like wildfire. But more likely, it's maybe a decade later that somebody figures out how to do it and make money."},"2":{"wt":"James Grimmelmann, New York Law School professor<ref name=NYT010613>{{cite news|last=Lewin|first=Tamar|title=Students Rush to Web Classes, but Profits May Be Much Later|url=https://www.nytimes.com/2013/01/07/education/massive-open-online-courses-prove-popular-if-not-lucrative-yet.html|access-date=6 March 2013|newspaper=[[The New York Times]]|date=6 January 2013|archive-date=31 July 2017|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170731173122/https://www.nytimes.com/2013/01/07/education/massive-open-online-courses-prove-popular-if-not-lucrative-yet.html|url-status=live}}</ref> "}},"i":0}}]}'/><blockquote class="templatequote " about="#mwt389"><p>No one's got the model that's going to work yet. I expect all the current ventures to fail, because the expectations are too high. People think something will catch on like wildfire. But more likely, it's maybe a decade later that somebody figures out how to do it and make money.</p></blockquote><div class="templatequotecite" about="#mwt389" id="mwA30"><p style="display: inline; padding-left: 2.3em;">—<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>James Grimmelmann, New York Law School professor<sup about="#mwt393" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT010613_121-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT010613"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYT010613-121"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT010613-121" id="mwA34"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA38"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4A">[</span>121<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4E">]</span></span></a></sup><span style="display:none" data-plural="0"></span></p></div> <p id="mwA4I">Principles of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Openness" title="Openness" id="mwA4M">openness</a> inform the creation, structure and operation of MOOCs. The extent to which practices of Open Design in educational technology<sup about="#mwt398" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-122" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-122"}}'><a href="#cite_note-122" id="mwA4Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA4U"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4Y">[</span>122<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA4c">]</span></span></a></sup> are applied vary.</p> <table class="wikitable" style="float:right;" id="mwA4g"> <caption id="mwA4k">Attributes of major MOOC providers,<sup about="#mwt400" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-123" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-123"}}'><a href="#cite_note-123" id="mwA4o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA4s"><span class="cite-bracket" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-124"}}'><a href="#cite_note-124" id="mwA44"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA48"><span <th id="mwA6A">Coursera</th> <td style="background:#FFC7C7;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-no" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt407" id="mwA6E"
