"]}'>Partial</td><td style="background:#9EFF9E;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-yes" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt409" id="mwA6M" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"yes","href":"./Template:Yes"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>Yes</td><td style="background:#FFB; color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center; " class="table-partial" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt410" id="mwA6Q" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"partial","href":"./Template:Partial"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'>Partial</td></tr> <tr id="mwA6U"> <th id="mwA6Y">Udacity</th> <td style="background:#FFC7C7;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-no" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt411" id="mwA6c" data-mw='{"parts":["| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"no","href":"./Template:No"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>No</td><td style="background:#FFB; color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center; " class="table-partial" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt412" id="mwA6g" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"partial","href":"./Template:Partial"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>Partial</td><td style="background:#9EFF9E;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-yes" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt413" id="mwA6k" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"yes","href":"./Template:Yes"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>Yes</td><td style="background:#FFB; color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center; " class="table-partial" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt414" id="mwA6o" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"partial","href":"./Template:Partial"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'>Partial</td></tr> <tr id="mwA6s"> <th id="mwA6w">Udemy</th> <td style="background:#FFC7C7;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-no" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt415" id="mwA60" data-mw='{"parts":["| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"no","href":"./Template:No"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>No</td><td style="background:#FFB; color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center; " class="table-partial" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt416" id="mwA64" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"partial","href":"./Template:Partial"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>Partial</td><td style="background:#9EFF9E;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-yes" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt417" id="mwA68" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"yes","href":"./Template:Yes"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>Yes</td><td style="background:#FFB; color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center; " class="table-partial" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt418" id="mwA7A" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"partial","href":"./Template:Partial"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'>Partial</td></tr> <tr id="mwA7E"> <th id="mwA7I">P2PU</th> <td style="background:#9EFF9E;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-yes" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt419" id="mwA7M" data-mw='{"parts":["| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"yes","href":"./Template:Yes"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>Yes</td><td style="background:#9EFF9E;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-yes" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt420" id="mwA7Q" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"yes","href":"./Template:Yes"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>Yes</td><td style="background:#FFC7C7;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-no" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt421" id="mwA7U" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"no","href":"./Template:No"},"params":{},"i":0}}," "]}'>No</td><td style="background:#FFC7C7;color:black;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center;" class="table-no" typeof="mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt422" id="mwA7Y" data-mw='{"parts":["|| ",{"template":{"target":{"wt":"no","href":"./Template:No"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'>No</td></tr> </tbody></table> <section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwA7c" aria-labelledby="Fee_opportunities"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Fee_opportunities">Fee opportunities</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=14" title="Edit section: Fee opportunities"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwA7g">In the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freemium" title="Freemium" id="mwA7k">freemium</a> business model, the basic product – the course content – is given away free. "Charging for content would be a tragedy", said Andrew Ng. But "premium" services such as certification or placement would be charged a fee – however financial aids are given in some cases.<sup about="#mwt425" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-SA31313_50-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"SA31313"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-SA31313-50"}}'><a href="#cite_note-SA31313-50" id="mwA7o"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA7s"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA7w">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA70">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwA74">Course developers could charge licensing fees for educational institutions that use its materials. Introductory or "gateway" courses and some remedial courses may earn the most fees. Free introductory courses may attract new students to follow-on fee-charging classes. Blended courses supplement MOOC material with face-to-face instruction. Providers can charge employers for recruiting its students. Students may be able to pay to take a proctored exam to earn transfer credit at a degree-granting university, or for certificates of completion.<sup about="#mwt426" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT010613_121-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT010613"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT010613-121" id="mwA78"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA8A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8E">[</span>121<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8I">]</span></span></a></sup> Udemy allows teachers to sell online courses, with the course creators keeping 70–85% of the proceeds and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intellectual_property_rights" title="Intellectual property rights" class="mw-redirect" id="mwA8M">intellectual property rights</a>.<sup about="#mwt429" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-125" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-125"}}'><a href="#cite_note-125" id="mwA8Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA8U"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8Y">[</span>125<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8c">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwA8g">Coursera found that students who paid $30 to $90 were substantially more likely to finish the course. The fee was ostensibly for the company's identity-verification program, which confirms that they took and passed a course.<sup about="#mwt430" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-wsj1013_54-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"wsj1013"}}'><a href="#cite_note-wsj1013-54" id="mwA8k"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA8o"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8s">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA8w">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <table class="wikitable" id="mwA80"> <caption id="mwA84">Overview of potential revenue sources for three MOOC providers<sup about="#mwt433" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-TechCrunch_126-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"TechCrunch"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-TechCrunch-126"}}'><a href="#cite_note-TechCrunch-126" id="mwA88"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA9A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA9E">[</span>126<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA9I">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt434" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-CETIS_127-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"CETIS"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-CETIS-127"}}'><a href="#cite_note-CETIS-127" id="mwA9M"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA9Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA9U">[</span>127<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA9Y">]</span></span></a></sup></caption> <tbody id="mwA9c"><tr id="mwA9g"> <th id="mwA9k">edX</th> <th id="mwA9o">Coursera</th> <th id="mwA9s">Udacity</th></tr> <tr id="mwA9w"> <td id="mwA90"> <ul id="mwA94"><li id="mwA98">Certification</li> <li id="mwA-A">College credits</li> <li id="mwA-E">Human tutoring or assignment marking</li> <li id="mwA-I">Financial aid</li> <li id="mwA-M">Proctored examinations</li></ul></td> <td id="mwA-Q"> <ul id="mwA-U"><li id="mwA-Y">Certification</li> <li id="mwA-c">Secure assessments</li> <li id="mwA-g">Employee recruitment</li> <li id="mwA-k">Applicant screening</li> <li id="mwA-o">Human tutoring or assignment marking</li> <li id="mwA-s">Enterprises pay to run their own training courses</li> <li id="mwA-w">Sponsorships</li> <li id="mwA-0">Tuition fees</li> <li id="mwA-4">Transcript services (not disclosed to students yet)</li></ul></td> <td id="mwA-8"> <ul id="mwA_A"><li id="mwA_E">Certification</li> <li id="mwA_I">Employers paying to recruit talented students</li> <li id="mwA_M">Students' résumés and job match services</li> <li id="mwA_Q">Sponsored high-tech skills courses</li></ul></td></tr> </tbody></table> <p id="mwA_U">In February 2013, the American Council on Education (ACE) recommended that its members provide transfer credit from a few MOOC courses, though even the universities who deliver the courses had said that they would not.<sup about="#mwt437" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-WSJ020713_128-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"WSJ020713"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-WSJ020713-128"}}'><a href="#cite_note-WSJ020713-128" id="mwA_Y"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA_c"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_g">[</span>128<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_k">]</span></span></a></sup> The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wisconsin" title="University of Wisconsin" class="mw-redirect" id="mwA_o">University of Wisconsin</a> offered multiple, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Competency-based_learning" title="Competency-based learning" id="mwA_s">competency-based</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor's_degree" title="Bachelor's degree" id="mwA_w">bachelor's</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master's_degree" title="Master's degree" id="mwA_0">master's</a> degrees starting Fall 2013, the first public university to do so on a system-wide basis. The university encouraged students to take online-courses such as MOOCs and complete assessment tests at the university to receive credit.<sup about="#mwt438" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-no_takers_129-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"no takers"}}'><a href="#cite_note-no_takers-129" id="mwA_4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA_8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAA">[</span>129<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAE">]</span></span></a></sup> As of 2013 few students had applied for college credit for MOOC classes.<sup about="#mwt441" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-130" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-130"}}'><a href="#cite_note-130" id="mwBAI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBAM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAQ">[</span>130<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAU">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_State_University-Global_Campus" title="Colorado State University-Global Campus" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBAY">Colorado State University-Global Campus</a> received no applications in the year after they offered the option.<sup about="#mwt446" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-no_takers_129-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"no takers"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-no_takers-129"}}'><a href="#cite_note-no_takers-129" id="mwBAc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBAg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAk">[</span>129<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBAo">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwBAs">Academic Partnerships is a company that helps public universities move their courses online. According to its chairman, Randy Best, "We started it, frankly, as a campaign to grow enrollment. But 72 to 84 percent of those who did the first course came back and paid to take the second course."<sup about="#mwt448" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-NYT012313_131-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"NYT012313"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-NYT012313-131"}}'><a href="#cite_note-NYT012313-131" id="mwBAw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBA0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBA4">[</span>131<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBA8">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwBBA">While Coursera takes a larger cut of any revenue generated – but requires no minimum payment – the not-for-profit edX has a minimum required payment from course providers, but takes a smaller cut of any revenues, tied to the amount of support required for each course.<sup about="#mwt451" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-132" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-132"}}'><a href="#cite_note-132" id="mwBBE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBBI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBBM">[</span>132<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBBQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section></section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwBBU" aria-labelledby="Benefits"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Benefits">Benefits</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=15" title="Edit section: Benefits"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <section data-mw-section-id="16" id="mwBBY" aria-labelledby="Improving_access_to_higher_education"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Improving_access_to_higher_education">Improving access to higher education</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=16" title="Edit section: Improving access to higher education"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBBc">MOOCs are regarded by many as an important tool to widen access to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education" title="Higher education" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBBg">higher education</a> (HE) for millions of people, including those in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developing_country" title="Developing country" id="mwBBk">developing world</a>, and ultimately enhance their quality of life.<sup about="#mwt454" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-CFP_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"CFP"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-CFP-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-CFP-2" id="mwBBo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBBs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBBw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBB0">]</span></span></a></sup> MOOCs may be regarded as contributing to the democratisation of HE, not only locally or regionally but globally as well. MOOCs can help democratise content and make knowledge reachable for everyone. Students are able to access complete courses offered by universities all over the world, something previously unattainable. With the availability of affordable technologies, MOOCs increase access to an extraordinary number of courses offered by world-renowned institutions and teachers.<sup about="#mwt457" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_133-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:0-133"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-133" id="mwBB4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBB8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBCA">[</span>133<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBCE">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="17" id="mwBCI" aria-labelledby="Providing_an_affordable_alternative_to_formal_education"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Providing_an_affordable_alternative_to_formal_education">Providing an affordable alternative to formal education</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=17" title="Edit section: Providing an affordable alternative to formal education"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBCM">The costs of tertiary education continue to increase because institutions tend to bundle too many services. With MOOCs, some of these services can be transferred to other suitable players in the public or private sector.<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwBCQ"> </span>MOOCs are for large numbers of participants, can be accessed by anyone anywhere as long as they have an Internet connection, are open to everyone without entry qualifications and offer a full/complete course experience online for free.<sup about="#mwt458" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-134" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-134"}}'><a href="#cite_note-134" id="mwBCU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBCY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBCc">[</span>134<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBCg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt459" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_133-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-133" id="mwBCk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBCo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBCs">[</span>133<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBCw">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="18" id="mwBC0" aria-labelledby="Sustainable_development_goals"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Sustainable_development_goals">Sustainable development goals</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=18" title="Edit section: Sustainable development goals"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt460" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBC4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Sustainable Development Goals"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1353705441" about="#mwt461" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt460" id="mwBC8">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_Development_Goals" title="Sustainable Development Goals">Sustainable Development Goals</a></div> <p id="mwBDA">MOOCs can be seen as a form of open education offered for free through online platforms. The (initial) philosophy of MOOCs is to open up quality higher education to a wider audience. As such, MOOCs are an important tool to achieve goal 4 of the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development.<sup about="#mwt462" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_133-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-133" id="mwBDE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBDI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBDM">[</span>133<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBDQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="19" id="mwBDU" aria-labelledby="Offers_a_flexible_learning_schedule"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Offers_a_flexible_learning_schedule">Offers a flexible learning schedule</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=19" title="Edit section: Offers a flexible learning schedule"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBDY">Certain lectures, videos, and tests through MOOCs can be accessed at any time compared to scheduled class times. By allowing learners to complete their coursework in their own time, this provides flexibility to learners based on their own personal schedules.<sup about="#mwt465" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-hbr.org_135-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"hbr.org"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-hbr.org-135"}}'><a href="#cite_note-hbr.org-135" id="mwBDc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBDg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBDk">[</span>135<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBDo">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt466" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_133-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-133" id="mwBDs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBDw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBD0">[</span>133<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBD4">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="20" id="mwBD8" aria-labelledby="Online_collaboration"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Online_collaboration">Online collaboration</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=20" title="Edit section: Online collaboration"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBEA">The learning environments of MOOCs make it easier for learners across the globe to work together on common goals. Instead of having to physically meet one another, online collaboration creates partnerships among learners. While time zones may have an effect on the hours that learners communicate, projects, assignments, and more can be completed to incorporate the skills and resources that different learners offer no matter where they are located.<sup about="#mwt467" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-hbr.org_135-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"hbr.org"}}'><a href="#cite_note-hbr.org-135" id="mwBEE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBEI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBEM">[</span>135<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBEQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt468" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_133-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-133" id="mwBEU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBEY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBEc">[</span>133<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBEg">]</span></span></a></sup> Distance and collaboration can benefit learners who may have struggled with traditionally more individual learning goals, including learning how to write.<sup about="#mwt471" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-136" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-136"}}'><a href="#cite_note-136" id="mwBEk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBEo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBEs">[</span>136<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBEw">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section></section><section data-mw-section-id="21" id="mwBE0" aria-labelledby="Criticisms_and_challenges"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Criticisms_and_challenges">Criticisms and challenges</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=21" title="Edit section: Criticisms and challenges"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt472" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBE4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Quote box","href":"./Template:Quote_box"},"params":{"align":{"wt":"right"},"width":{"wt":"50%"},"author":{"wt":"[[San Jose State University]] open letter to [[Michael Sandel]], 2013<ref name=\"Dear Sandel\">{{cite news|title=San Jose State to Michael Sandel: Keep your MOOC off our campus|url=http://www.boston.com/bostonglobe/ideas/brainiac/2013/05/san_jose_state.html|access-date=7 May 2013|newspaper=[[The Boston Globe]]|date=3 May 2013|archive-date=9 May 2013|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130509020141/http://www.boston.com/bostonglobe/ideas/brainiac/2013/05/san_jose_state.html|url-status=dead}}</ref>"},"quote":{"wt":"Should one-size-fits-all vendor-designed blended courses become the norm, we fear two classes of universities will be created: one, well-funded colleges and universities in which privileged students get their own real professor; the other, financially stressed private and public universities in which students watch a bunch of video-taped lectures."}},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1224211176" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt473" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Quote_box/styles.css"}}'>.mw-parser-output .quotebox{background-color:#F9F9F9;border:1px solid #aaa;box-sizing:border-box;padding:10px;font-size:88%;max-width:100%}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft{margin:.5em 1.4em .8em 0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright{margin:.5em 0 .8em 1.4em}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.centered{overflow:hidden;position:relative;margin:.5em auto .8em auto}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft span,.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright span{font-style:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox>blockquote{margin:0;padding:0;border-left:0;font-family:inherit;font-size:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-title{text-align:center;font-size:110%;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote>:first-child{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote:last-child>:last-child{margin-bottom:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:before{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" “ ";vertical-align:-45%;line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:after{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" ” ";line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .left-aligned{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .right-aligned{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .center-aligned{text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quote-title,.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quotebox-quote{display:block}.mw-parser-output .quotebox cite{display:block;font-style:normal}@media screen and (max-width:640px){.mw-parser-output .quotebox{width:100%!important;margin:0 0 .8em!important;float:none!important}}</style></span><div class="quotebox pullquote floatright " style=" width:50%; ; " about="#mwt472" id="mwBE8"> <blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned " style=" "> <p>Should one-size-fits-all vendor-designed blended courses become the norm, we fear two classes of universities will be created: one, well-funded colleges and universities in which privileged students get their own real professor; the other, financially stressed private and public universities in which students watch a bunch of video-taped lectures.</p> </blockquote> <div style="padding-bottom: 0; padding-top: 0.5em"><cite class="left-aligned" style="">—<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose_State_University" title="San Jose State University">San Jose State University</a> open letter to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Sandel" title="Michael Sandel">Michael Sandel</a>, 2013<sup about="#mwt476" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Dear_Sandel_137-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Dear Sandel"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Dear_Sandel-137"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Dear_Sandel-137" id="mwBFA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBFE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBFI">[</span>137<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBFM">]</span></span></a></sup></cite></div> </div> <p id="mwBFQ">MOOCs have received a significant amount of criticism, especially during the early 2010s when it received a significant amount of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gartner_hype_cycle" title="Gartner hype cycle" id="mwBFU">hype</a> among educators.<sup about="#mwt481" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-138" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-138"}}'><a href="#cite_note-138" id="mwBFY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBFc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBFg">[</span>138<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBFk">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <p id="mwBFo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cary_Nelson" title="Cary Nelson" id="mwBFs">Cary Nelson</a>, former president of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Association_of_University_Professors" title="American Association of University Professors" id="mwBFw">American Association of University Professors</a> claimed that MOOCs are not a reliable means of supplying credentials, stating that "It’s fine to put lectures online, but this plan only degrades degree programs if it plans to substitute for them." Sandra Schroeder, chair of the Higher Education Program and Policy Council for the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Federation_of_Teachers" title="American Federation of Teachers" id="mwBF0">American Federation of Teachers</a> expressed concern that "These students are not likely to succeed without the structure of a strong and sequenced academic program."<sup about="#mwt484" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-139" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-139"}}'><a href="#cite_note-139" id="mwBF4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBF8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBGA">[</span>139<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBGE">]</span></span></a></sup></p> <section data-mw-section-id="22" id="mwBGI" aria-labelledby="Certification_and_credentialing"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Certification_and_credentialing">Certification and credentialing</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=22" title="Edit section: Certification and credentialing"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBGM">MOOCs frequently offer digital <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_certificate" title="Academic certificate" id="mwBGQ">certificates</a>, specializations, and so-called "micro-credentials" upon successful completion of course requirements.<sup about="#mwt487" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Rivas2020_140-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Rivas2020"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Rivas2020-140"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Rivas2020-140" id="mwBGU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBGY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBGc">[</span>140<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBGg">]</span></span></a></sup> However, the value of such credentials within labor markets remains a subject of debate, particularly when compared to traditional educational qualifications. In 2020, respondents who used the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_Mechanical_Turk" title="Amazon Mechanical Turk" id="mwBGk">Mechanical Turk</a> crowdsourcing service preferred freelance web developers with traditional degrees over a MOOC credential.<sup about="#mwt488" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Rivas2020_140-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Rivas2020"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Rivas2020-140" id="mwBGo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBGs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBGw">[</span>140<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBG0">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="23" id="mwBG4" aria-labelledby="Completion_rates"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Completion_rates">Completion rates</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=23" title="Edit section: Completion rates"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBG8">Completion rates in MOOCs are consistently very low. In 2012, MOOCs provided by Harvard and MIT had completion rates of 22% on average.<sup about="#mwt492" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-141" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-141"}}'><a href="#cite_note-141" id="mwBHA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBHE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBHI">[</span>141<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBHM">]</span></span></a></sup></p> </section><section data-mw-section-id="24" id="mwBHQ" aria-labelledby="The_experience_of_English_language_learners_in_MOOCs"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="The_experience_of_English_language_learners_in_MOOCs">The experience of English language learners in MOOCs</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=24" title="Edit section: The experience of English language learners in MOOCs"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBHU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_of_instruction" title="Language of instruction" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBHY">Language of instruction</a> is one of the major barriers that <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English-language_learner" title="English-language learner" id="mwBHc">English-language learners</a> (ELLs) face in MOOCs. In recent estimates, almost 75% of MOOC courses are presented in the English language, however, native English speakers <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_languages_by_number_of_native_speakers" title="List of languages by number of native speakers" id="mwBHg">are a minority</a> among the world's population.<sup about="#mwt495" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-142" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-142"}}'><a href="#cite_note-142" id="mwBHk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBHo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBHs">[</span>142<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBHw">]</span></span></a></sup> This issue is mitigated by the increasing popularity of English as a global language, and therefore has more second language speakers than any other language in the world. This barrier has encouraged content developers and other MOOC stakeholders to develop content in other popular languages to increase MOOC access. However, research studies show that some ELLs prefer to take MOOCs in English, despite the language challenges, as it promotes their goals of economic, social, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographic_mobility" title="Geographic mobility" id="mwBH0">geographic mobility</a>.<sup about="#mwt498" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-143" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-143"}}'><a href="#cite_note-143" id="mwBH4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBH8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBIA">[</span>143<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBIE">]</span></span></a></sup> This emphasizes the need to not only provide MOOC content in other languages, but also to develop English-language interventions for ELLs who participate in English MOOCs.</p> <p id="mwBII">Areas that ELLs particularly struggle with in English MOOCs include MOOC content without corresponding visual supporting materials<sup about="#mwt501" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-144" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-144"}}'><a href="#cite_note-144" id="mwBIM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBIQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBIU">[</span>144<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBIY">]</span></span></a></sup> (e.g., an instructor narrating instruction without text support in the background), or their hesitation to participate in MOOC discussion forums.<sup about="#mwt504" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Kulkarni_1116–1128_145-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Kulkarni 1116–1128"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Kulkarni_1116–1128-145"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Kulkarni_1116–1128-145" id="mwBIc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBIg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBIk">[</span>145<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBIo">]</span></span></a></sup> Active participation in MOOC discussion forums has been found to improve students grades, their engagement, and leads to lower dropout rates,<sup about="#mwt507" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-146" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-146"}}'><a href="#cite_note-146" id="mwBIs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBIw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBI0">[</span>146<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBI4">]</span></span></a></sup> however, ELLs are more likely to be spectators than active contributors in discussion forums.<sup about="#mwt508" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Kulkarni_1116–1128_145-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Kulkarni 1116–1128"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Kulkarni_1116–1128-145" id="mwBI8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBJA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJE">[</span>145<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJI">]</span></span></a></sup><span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwBJM"> </span></p> <p id="mwBJQ">Researching studies show a “complex mix of affective, socio-cultural, and educational factors” that are inhibitors to their active participation in discussion forums.<sup about="#mwt511" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-147" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-147"}}'><a href="#cite_note-147" id="mwBJU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBJY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJc">[</span>147<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJg">]</span></span></a></sup> As expected, English as the language of communication poses both linguistic and cultural challenges for ELLs, and they may not be confident in their English language communication abilities.<sup about="#mwt514" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-148" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-148"}}'><a href="#cite_note-148" id="mwBJk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBJo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJs">[</span>148<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJw">]</span></span></a></sup> Discussion forums may also be an uncomfortable means of communication especially for ELLs from Confucian cultures, where disagreement and arguing one’s points are often viewed as confrontational, and harmony is promoted.<sup about="#mwt517" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-149" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-149"}}'><a href="#cite_note-149" id="mwBJ0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBJ4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJ8">[</span>149<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBKA">]</span></span></a></sup> Therefore, while ELLs may be perceived as being uninterested in participating, research studies show that they do not show the same hesitation in face to face discourse.<sup about="#mwt520" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:2_150-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":2"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:2-150"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:2-150" id="mwBKE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBKI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBKM">[</span>150<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBKQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt523" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:3_151-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":3"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:3-151"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:3-151" id="mwBKU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBKY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBKc">[</span>151<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBKg">]</span></span></a></sup> Finally, ELLs may come from high power distance cultures,<sup about="#mwt526" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-152" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-152"}}'><a href="#cite_note-152" id="mwBKk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBKo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBKs">[</span>152<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBKw">]</span></span></a></sup> where teachers are regarded as authority figures, and the culture of back and forth conversations between teachers and students is not a cultural norm.<sup about="#mwt527" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:2_150-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:2-150" id="mwBK0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBK4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBK8">[</span>150<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBLA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt528" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:3_151-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:3-151" id="mwBLE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBLI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBLM">[</span>151<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBLQ">]</span></span></a></sup> As a result, discussion forums with active participation from the instructors may cause discomfort and prevent participation of students from such cultures.</p> </section></section><section data-mw-section-id="25" id="mwBLU" aria-labelledby="Curation"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Curation">Curation</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Massive_open_online_course&action=edit&section=25" title="Edit section: Curation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div> <p id="mwBLY"><span class="anchor" id="Open_Culture" about="#mwt529" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"anchor","href":"./Template:Anchor"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Open Culture"}},"i":0}}]}'></span><i id="mwBLc">Open Culture</i>, not affiliated with Stanford University, founded in 2006 by Dan Coleman, the Director and Associate Dean of Stanford University's Continuing Education Program, aggregates and curates free MOOCs, as well as free cultural and educational media.<sup about="#mwt533" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-153" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-153"}}'><a href="#cite_note-153" id="mwBLg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBLk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBLo">[</span>153<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBLs">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt536" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-154" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-154"}}'><a href="#cite_note-154" id="mwBLw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBL0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBL4">[</span>154<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBL8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt539" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-155" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-155"}}'><a href="#cite_note-155" id="mwBMA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBME"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMI">[</span>155<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt542" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-156" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-156"}}'><a href="#cite_note-156" id="mwBMQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBMU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMY">[</span>156<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMc">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt545" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-157" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-157"}}'><a href="#cite_note-157" id="mwBMg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBMk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMo">[</span>157<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMs">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt548" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-158" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-158"}}'><a href="#cite_note-158" id="mwBMw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBM0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBM4">[</span>158<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBM8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt551" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-159" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-159"}}'><a href="#cite_note-159" id="mwBNA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBNE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBNI">[</span>159<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBNM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt554" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-160" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-160"}}'><a href="#cite_note-160" id="mwBNQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBNU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBNY">[</span>160<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBNc">]</span></span></a></sup><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Citation_overkill" about="#mwt530" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBNg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Excessive citations inline","href":"./Template:Excessive_citations_inline"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"May 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_tagged_with_the_inline_citation_overkill_template_from_May_2026" about="#mwt530"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt530" id="mwBNk"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#Bundling_citations" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This claim has too many footnotes for reading to be smooth. (May 2026)">excessive citations</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup> C.
